DONE — restricted theorem PASS; finite tests support the algebra within their limited scope. G2 classifies the lemma as an explicit elementary corollary of standard central random-walk Fourier theory. No noncentral-walk extension is certified.

# Two isolated referees and G2 audit of the E1 restricted theorem

Task 019, 2026-09-26. The two referees received fresh contexts containing only the supplied theorem, its proof, and the normalization restatement. Neither read the campaign, the other referee, or the G2 result during that work. A third agent separately checked primary literature. The root compared the completed outputs afterward. All are same-model checks, not cross-vendor review or kernel formalization.

**Verdicts:** referee A PASS; referee B PASS as a limited numerical/adversarial check, with no counterexample in the theorem's scope. The central-measure lemma can be used with its exact hypotheses. Its application to the actual noncentral Zaremba walks remains a separate open task. The G2 evidence does not support presenting this lemma as a new general flattening principle or as removal of BSG for arbitrary measures.

## 1. The statement that was actually checked

Let G=SL2(F_p), p>=5, D=p(p^2-1), Z={+/-I}, and t(mu)=||mu||_2^2-1/D in counting measure. For every conjugacy-invariant probability measure, without a symmetry assumption,

\[
 t(\mu*\mu)\le[\mu(Z)+5p^{-1/2}]^2t(\mu).                \tag{1}
\]

If mu(Z)<=p^{-a}, 0<c<min(a,1/2), and

\[
 p\ge\max\{5,2^{1/(a-c)},10^{1/(1/2-c)}\},
\]

then t(mu*mu)<=p^{-2c}t(mu), with coefficient one and no restriction on the size of t(mu). For a=tau/6>=1/30 and c=1/120, p>=2^40 suffices. The gain is p^{-1/60}; it equals K_*^{-c_K} for K_*=p^{tau/6} exactly when c_K=12c/tau. These are central-measure onsets and exponents, not Zaremba onsets or a statement about arbitrary measures.

## 2. Isolated referee A — PASS

**Representation degree.** Every nontrivial irreducible complex representation of SL2(F_p) has degree at least (p-1)/2, including representations nontrivial on the center. The referee verified this against [Donald L. White, Theorem B](https://www.math.kent.edu/~white/papers/sl2.pdf), specialized to SL2(F_p); the possible degrees are 1,(p-1)/2,(p+1)/2,p-1,p,p+1, with the sole degree-one character principal for p>=5.

It also admits a short independent check: a nontrivial irreducible is nontrivial on the upper unipotent subgroup, since its conjugates generate G. A nonzero additive character therefore occurs. Conjugation by diag(s,s^{-1}) produces all (p-1)/2 square multiples of that character, forcing that many distinct weight spaces.

**Centralizers and Fourier normalization.** A nonscalar matrix's commuting algebra is F_p[g]. Its determinant-one units have sizes p-1, p+1, or 2p in the split, nonsplit, or repeated-root case. In the last case they are sI+tN with s=+/-1. Character-column orthogonality consequently gives

\[
 \frac{|\chi_\rho(g)|}{d_\rho}
 \le\frac{2\sqrt{2p}}{p-1}
 \le3\sqrt2\,p^{-1/2}<5p^{-1/2}\quad(g\notin Z).
\]

For a central measure, Schur gives muhat(rho)=z_rho I. Counting-measure Plancherel is exactly

\[
 t(\mu)=D^{-1}\sum_{\rho\ne1}d_\rho^2|z_\rho|^2,
 \qquad t(\mu*\mu)=D^{-1}\sum_{\rho\ne1}d_\rho^2|z_\rho|^4.
\]

The scalars may be complex when the law is not symmetric; |z^2|^2=|z|^4 still proves the argument. No symmetry hypothesis is missing. The uniform law has t=0 and satisfies the conclusion directly.

**Adversarial subgroup averages.** For any subgroup H, let eta_H be the conjugacy average of its uniform probability law. Then

\[
 \eta_H(Z)=|H\cap Z|/|H|,\qquad
 z_\rho(\eta_H)=\dim(V_\rho^H)/d_\rho.
\]

The same character bound controls this scalar. This covers Borel groups, torus normalizers, and all existing exceptional subgroups without a classification-based gap. Convex mixtures with central atoms or uniform measure preserve the bound.

As an exact Borel example, the action on G/B=P^1(F_p) is doubly transitive, so its permutation representation is 1 plus one irreducible representation of dimension p. Frobenius reciprocity shows that the only nonzero nontrivial scalar of eta_B is 1/p. Its exact energy contraction ratio is therefore 1/p^2. For mu_theta=(1-theta)u+theta delta_I, the exact ratio is theta^2 when theta>0, and the center mass is theta+(1-theta)2/D. Laws supported entirely on Z may have ratio 1; the theorem explicitly retains that obstruction.

**Onsets and coefficient.** The two displayed threshold conditions make p^{-a} and 5p^{-1/2} each at most (1/2)p^{-c}, giving coefficient exactly one. For c=1/120, a>=1/30 gives a-c>=1/40 and onset 2^40. At tau=1/4 one instead has a-c=1/30 and onset 2^30. The other threshold satisfies 10^{120/59}<10^3<2^10, so it is smaller in both cases. The all-proper-subgroup-coset cap implies the required center mass bound by taking H=Z; the proof does not need the other cosets.

The referee found no required mathematical repair. It verified the primary degree source and the proof algebra, performed no numerical calculation, read no local campaign files, and made no novelty claim.

## 3. G2 — printed identity and standard specialization

The decisive prior statement is explicit in Nathanael Berestycki's *Mixing Times of Markov Chains: Techniques and Examples*, dated 22 November 2016. Lemma 6.2 gives scalar Fourier transforms for class functions; Theorem 6.8 and Corollary 6.5 give

\[
 |G|\|P^{*k}-U\|_2^2=\sum_{\rho\ne1}d_\rho^2|\lambda_\rho|^{2k}
\]

for central probabilities. Symmetry is not assumed; the subsequent remark explains that it makes the scalars real. Comparing k=2 with k=1 immediately gives contraction by max_{rho!=1}|lambda_rho|^2. That one-step formulation is a transparent corollary of the printed identities. [Author-hosted notes, sections 6.4–6.5](https://homepage.univie.ac.at/nathanael.berestycki/wp-content/uploads/2022/05/mixing3.pdf).

The SL2 ingredients are likewise printed: [Helfgott, Proposition 5.5](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1303.0239#page=32) gives the degree bound; [Igusa, Theorem 2.35 and Corollary 2.36](https://people.brandeis.edu/~igusa/Math101bS07/Math101b_notesD.pdf) give column orthogonality; [Murnaghan's SL2 chapter, pp.2 and 9](https://www.math.toronto.edu/fiona/courses/mat445/ch3.pdf) computes the noncentral centralizers. Their substitution gives

\[
 |\lambda_\rho|\le\mu(Z)+(1-\mu(Z))\frac{2\sqrt{2p}}{p-1}
 \le\mu(Z)+5p^{-1/2}.
\]

For p>=5 the coefficient before p^{-1/2} in the middle term is at most 5/sqrt2, so 5 is a convenient nonsharp rounding. The stated onset is elementary arithmetic applied to this bound. The G2 search did not locate the exact coefficient/exponent/onset packaging; that fact supports no priority claim.

The requested broader literature has different statements:

- [Bourgain–Gamburd, Proposition 2, (16)–(20)](https://annals.math.princeton.edu/wp-content/uploads/annals-v167-n2-p07.pdf#page=6) proves flattening for symmetric measures that need not be central, under an infinity-norm cap, an L2 lower bound, and subgroup escape. Equation (17) uses the infinity norm. The resulting exponent depends on a parameter gamma; this is a broader mechanism and is not needed for (1).
- [Babai–Nikolov–Pyber, Theorem 2.1, (2.3)](https://people.cs.uchicago.edu/~laci/papers/bnp-soda08.pdf) gives ||X*Y-U||_2<=sqrt(|G|/m)||X-U||_2||Y-U||_2, with m their minimum nontrivial real representation dimension. For equal laws it gives t(mu*mu)<= (|G|/m)t(mu)^2, not the central-mass character-ratio formula.
- [Gowers, Lemma 3.2 and Theorem 3.3](https://arxiv.org/pdf/0710.3877) give a mean-zero convolution-operator bound and product mixing from minimum representation degree. These are quasirandomness results rather than the central character-ratio formulation.
- The original [Sarnak–Xue article](https://doi.org/10.1215/S0012-7094-91-06410-0) could not be read in usable full text. Bourgain–Gamburd credit it for the multiplicity/closed-walk spectral method; this is not evidence that (1) is absent from the original article. Helfgott's survey separately presents general flattening and the multiplicity method.

Recommended classification: **an explicit elementary SL2(F_p) corollary of standard central random-walk Fourier theory, column orthogonality, and Frobenius's degree bound.** Its proof can be relied on within its stated class. Neither the source audit nor either referee provides the missing noncentral-walk extension.

## 4. Isolated referee B — numerical record

This section was prepared from the supplied theorem and proof only. No campaign notes,
other referee reports, or previous contents of this path were read. The global-memory
recall tool was unavailable in this isolated tool catalog; no user notice was invented.
The parent explicitly authorized one dependency-free Node numerical process alongside
its one existing process, with a total cap of two numerical processes. No subagents,
Python, dependencies, cloud services, or paid API calls are used here.

## Scope and method

The test fixes a = 1/30 and c = 1/120. It enumerates SL(2,F_p) for
p = 3, 5, 7, 11, 13, 17, 31 and uses the exact conjugacy-class multiplication counts.
Every measure is nonnegative and conjugacy invariant. Class masses are rational;
convolution energies and their ratio are computed as exact BigInt fractions.
The comparisons against p^(-1/60), the center cap p^(-1/30), and the actual universal
bracket [mu(Z) + 5/sqrt(p)]^2 are also exact integer comparisons.

The classes are the two scalars, one class for each trace other than +/-2, and
two nonscalar classes at each exceptional trace. The exceptional classes use the
square class of the upper-right entry, or of minus the lower-left entry if needed.
Invariance under conjugation by both elementary unipotent generators is checked
for every enumerated matrix. Class sizes and class-algebra row sums are checked.
No symmetry assumption is imposed; nonsymmetric samples are counted explicitly.

The imposed hypothesis is ONLY mu(Z) <= p^(-1/30). No all-proper-subgroup-coset
condition is imposed or inferred. In fact, the script also checks the necessary
H=Z condition on every coset and labels samples which fail that stronger condition.
Passing this necessary condition does not establish the full all-coset condition.
Uncapped central controls are identified separately.

All sampled primes are below 2^40; p=3 also lies outside the proposition's p>=5
domain. Thus any failure of the p^(-1/60) target here is NOT a counterexample to
the supplied effective theorem. Finite checks cannot certify that theorem.

## Reproduction

Run the fenced JavaScript below using Node, with a 180-second CPU limit and 2 GiB
address-space limit. The tested invocation uses `--max-old-space-size=768`.
The script has an additional 150-second process CPU budget and writes its full
JSON data back into this same report. The seed is fixed. It requires only Node's
built-in `fs` module. The exact-ratio numerator and denominator are included for
every sample; decimal ratios are display aids only.

```javascript
'use strict';
const fs = require('fs');
const REPORT = '/work/campaigns/zaremba-M/phase1/REFEREE_E1.md';
const PRIMES = [3, 5, 7, 11, 13, 17, 31];
const CPU0 = process.cpuUsage();
const WALL0 = Date.now();
let seed = 0x019b2026;
function rng() {
  seed ^= seed << 13; seed ^= seed >>> 17; seed ^= seed << 5;
  return (seed >>> 0) / 4294967296;
}
function budget() {
  const u = process.cpuUsage(CPU0);
  if (u.user + u.system > 150000000) throw new Error('150-second CPU budget');
}
function assert(x, message) { if (!x) throw new Error(message); }
function gcd(a, b) { while (b) [a, b] = [b, a % b]; return a; }
function lcm(a, b) { return a / gcd(a, b) * b; }
function decimal(n, d) { return Number(n) / Number(d); }
function build(p) {
  const D = p * (p*p - 1), K = p + 4;
  const mod = x => ((x % p) + p) % p;
  const square = new Uint8Array(p), inv = new Int32Array(p);
  for (let x = 1; x < p; x++) square[x*x % p] = 1;
  for (let x = 1; x < p; x++) for (let y = 1; y < p; y++)
    if (x*y % p === 1) inv[x] = y;
  const traceIndex = new Int32Array(p).fill(-1);
  const labels = ['I', '-I'];
  for (let t = 0; t < p; t++) if (t !== 2 && t !== p-2) {
    traceIndex[t] = labels.length; labels.push('trace:' + t);
  }
  for (const t of [2, p-2]) for (const q of ['nonsquare', 'square'])
    labels.push('trace:' + t + ':' + q);
  const cls = (a,b,c,d) => {
    if (b === 0 && c === 0 && a === d) return a === 1 ? 0 : 1;
    const t = (a+d) % p;
    if (t !== 2 && t !== p-2) return traceIndex[t];
    return p + (t === 2 ? 0 : 2) + square[b || mod(-c)];
  };
  const entries = new Uint16Array(4*D), groupClass = new Uint16Array(D);
  const sizes = Array(K).fill(0), reps = Array(K);
  let pos = 0;
  function add(a,b,c,d) {
    const k = cls(a,b,c,d);
    assert(mod(a*d-b*c) === 1, 'determinant');
    assert(cls(mod(a+c),mod(b+d-a-c),c,mod(d-c)) === k, 'upper conjugation');
    assert(cls(mod(a-b),b,mod(a+c-b-d),mod(b+d)) === k, 'lower conjugation');
    entries.set([a,b,c,d], 4*pos); groupClass[pos] = k; pos++;
    sizes[k]++; if (!reps[k]) reps[k] = [a,b,c,d];
  }
  for (let a = 1; a < p; a++) for (let b = 0; b < p; b++)
    for (let c = 0; c < p; c++) add(a,b,c,mod((1+b*c)*inv[a]));
  for (let b = 1; b < p; b++) for (let d = 0; d < p; d++)
    add(0,b,mod(-inv[b]),d);
  assert(pos === D && sizes[0] === 1 && sizes[1] === 1, 'group size');
  for (let t = 0; t < p; t++) if (traceIndex[t] >= 0)
    assert(sizes[traceIndex[t]] === p*(p+(square[mod(t*t-4)] ? 1 : -1)), 'regular class size');
  for (let k = p; k < K; k++) assert(sizes[k] === (p*p-1)/2, 'unipotent size');
  const inverseClass = reps.map(([a,b,c,d]) => cls(d,mod(-b),mod(-c),a));
  const negativeClass = reps.map(([a,b,c,d]) => cls(mod(-a),mod(-b),mod(-c),mod(-d)));
  const counts = new Uint32Array(K*K*K);
  for (let k = 0; k < K; k++) {
    budget();
    const [A,B,C,E] = reps[k];
    for (let h = 0; h < D; h++) {
      const off=4*h, a=entries[off], b=entries[off+1], c=entries[off+2], d=entries[off+3];
      const j=cls(mod(d*A-b*C),mod(d*B-b*E),mod(-c*A+a*C),mod(-c*B+a*E));
      counts[(k*K+groupClass[h])*K+j]++;
    }
  }
  const sparse = [];
  for (let k=0; k<K; k++) {
    let total=0; const row=[];
    for (let i=0; i<K; i++) for (let j=0; j<K; j++) {
      const v=counts[(k*K+i)*K+j]; total+=v;
      if (v) row.push([i,j,BigInt(v)]);
      assert(v === counts[(k*K+j)*K+i], 'commutative central class algebra');
    }
    assert(total===D, 'class algebra row sum'); sparse.push(row);
  }
  let L=1n; for (const n of sizes) L=lcm(L,BigInt(n));
  return {p,D,K,sizes,reps,labels,inverseClass,negativeClass,sparse,L};
}
function evaluate(G, name, w) {
  budget();
  const {p,D,K,sizes,sparse,L,inverseClass,negativeClass}=G;
  assert(w.length===K && w.every(x=>Number.isSafeInteger(x)&&x>=0), 'weights');
  const W=BigInt(w.reduce((x,y)=>x+y,0)); assert(W>0n,'positive total');
  const z=BigInt(w[0]+w[1]), Q=W*L, Q2=Q*Q, Q4=Q2*Q2, bigD=BigInt(D);
  const a=w.map((x,k)=>BigInt(x)*(L/BigInt(sizes[k])));
  const b=sparse.map(row=>row.reduce((sum,[i,j,n])=>sum+n*a[i]*a[j],0n));
  assert(b.reduce((sum,x,k)=>sum+BigInt(sizes[k])*x,0n)===Q2,'convolution mass');
  const E1=bigD*a.reduce((sum,x,k)=>sum+BigInt(sizes[k])*x*x,0n)-Q2;
  const E2=bigD*b.reduce((sum,x,k)=>sum+BigInt(sizes[k])*x*x,0n)-Q4;
  assert(E1>=0n && E2>=0n,'nonnegative energy');
  const cap=BigInt(p)*z**30n<=W**30n;
  const symmetric=a.every((x,k)=>x===a[inverseClass[k]]);
  const maxZCosetNumerator=a.reduce((m,x,k)=>{
    const value=x+a[negativeClass[k]]; return value>m?value:m;
  },0n);
  const allZCosetsCap=BigInt(p)*maxZCosetNumerator**30n<=Q**30n;
  const basic={name,weights:w,totalWeight:W.toString(),centerMass:decimal(z,W),centerCap:cap,
    symmetric,allCenterCosetsCap:allZCosetsCap,maxCenterCosetMass:decimal(maxZCosetNumerator,Q),
    bracketDisplay:(decimal(z,W)+5/Math.sqrt(p))**2,targetDisplay:p**(-1/60)};
  if (E1===0n) {
    assert(E2===0n,'zero energy convolution');
    return {...basic,zeroEnergy:true,ratio:null,universalBracketPass:true,powerTargetPass:true};
  }
  const M=Q2*E1, common=gcd(E2,M), N=E2/common, Rden=M/common;
  // X = R - (z/W)^2 - 25/p. If X>0, compare X^2 to 100(z/W)^2/p.
  const Xn=N*W*W*BigInt(p)-Rden*z*z*BigInt(p)-25n*Rden*W*W;
  const universalBracketPass=Xn<=0n || Xn*Xn<=100n*z*z*Rden*Rden*W*W*BigInt(p);
  const powerTargetPass=BigInt(p)*N**60n<=Rden**60n;
  const ratio=decimal(N,Rden);
  assert(N<=Rden,'probability convolution L2 contraction');
  return {...basic,zeroEnergy:false,ratio,ratioNumerator:N.toString(),ratioDenominator:Rden.toString(),
    universalBracketPass,powerTargetPass};
}
function randomSplit(total,count) {
  const q=Array.from({length:count},()=>1+Math.floor(rng()*10000));
  const s=q.reduce((x,y)=>x+y,0), r=q.map(x=>Math.floor(total*x/s));
  let remainder=total-r.reduce((x,y)=>x+y,0);
  for (let i=0; remainder>0; i=(i+1)%count,remainder--) r[i]++;
  return r;
}
const data={status:'OPEN',role:'isolated referee B numerical',seed:'0x019b2026',a:'1/30',c:'1/120',
  squaredMassGain:'1/60',uniformOnset:'2^40',allCosetConditionImposed:false,
  arithmetic:'Exact integer class convolution and exact integer inequality comparisons',
  primes:[]};
for (const p of PRIMES) {
  const G=build(p), {K,labels,sizes}=G, tests=[];
  const add=(name,w)=>tests.push(evaluate(G,name,w));
  const unit=k=>Array.from({length:K},(_,j)=>Number(j===k));
  add('uniform-group',sizes);
  add('delta-I-control',unit(0)); add('delta-minus-I-control',unit(1));
  const zlaw=unit(0); zlaw[1]=1; add('uniform-center-control',zlaw);
  add('uniform-noncentral',sizes.map((x,k)=>k<2?0:x));
  const W=1000000, capBase=BigInt(W)**30n;
  let zcap=Math.floor(W*p**(-1/30));
  while (BigInt(p)*BigInt(zcap)**30n>capBase) zcap--;
  while (BigInt(p)*BigInt(zcap+1)**30n<=capBase) zcap++;
  for (let k=2; k<K; k++) {
    add('single-class:'+labels[k],unit(k));
    const w=Array(K).fill(0); w[0]=zcap; w[k]=W-zcap;
    add('cap-I-plus:'+labels[k],w);
    const v=w.slice(); v[0]=Math.floor(zcap/2); v[1]=zcap-v[0];
    add('cap-split-center-plus:'+labels[k],v);
  }
  for (let r=0; r<32; r++) {
    add('random-class-masses:'+r,Array.from({length:K},()=>1+Math.floor(rng()*10000)));
    const tail=randomSplit(W-zcap,K-2), s=Math.floor(rng()*(zcap+1));
    add('random-cap-center:'+r,[s,zcap-s,...tail]);
  }
  if (p===3) {
    const q8=Array(K).fill(0); q8[0]=1; q8[1]=1;
    q8[labels.indexOf('trace:0')]=6; add('uniform-Q8-p3-outside-domain',q8);
  }
  const capped=tests.filter(x=>x.centerCap), positive=tests.filter(x=>!x.zeroEnergy);
  const cappedPositive=capped.filter(x=>!x.zeroEnergy);
  const max=xs=>xs.reduce((a,b)=>a===null||b.ratio>a.ratio?b:a,null);
  const worst=max(cappedPositive);
  const summary={p,groupOrder:G.D,classes:K,samples:tests.length,centerCapped:capped.length,
    nonsymmetric:tests.filter(x=>!x.symmetric).length,
    cappedFailNecessaryCenterCoset: capped.filter(x=>!x.allCenterCosetsCap).map(x=>x.name),
    universalBracketFailures:tests.filter(x=>!x.universalBracketPass).map(x=>x.name),
    cappedPowerFailures:capped.filter(x=>!x.powerTargetPass).map(x=>x.name),
    controlPowerFailures:tests.filter(x=>!x.centerCap&&!x.powerTargetPass).map(x=>x.name),
    maxCappedRatio:worst.ratio,maxCappedRatioName:worst.name,
    minBracket:Math.min(...positive.map(x=>x.bracketDisplay)),
    maxRatioOverBracket:Math.max(...positive.map(x=>x.ratio/x.bracketDisplay)),
    target:p**(-1/60)};
  data.primes.push({summary,classLabels:labels,classSizes:sizes,tests});
  process.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(summary)+'\n');
}
data.constants={uniformFirstTerm:'2^40',tauQuarterFirstTerm:'2^30',
  noiseTermBelowBoth:10n**120n<2n**(30n*59n),
  cKAtTauQuarter:'2/5',cKAtTauFifth:'1/2',cPrimeAtTauQuarter:'1/5',cPrimeAtTauFifth:'1/4'};
data.cpuSeconds=Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(process.cpuUsage(CPU0)).map(([k,v])=>[k,v/1e6]));
data.wallSeconds=(Date.now()-WALL0)/1000;
data.peakRssKiB=process.resourceUsage().maxRSS;
const totals={samples:data.primes.reduce((s,x)=>s+x.summary.samples,0),
  universalBracketFailures:data.primes.flatMap(x=>x.summary.universalBracketFailures),
  cappedPowerFailures:data.primes.flatMap(x=>x.summary.cappedPowerFailures.map(name=>({p:x.summary.p,name}))),
  cpuSeconds:data.cpuSeconds,wallSeconds:data.wallSeconds,peakRssKiB:data.peakRssKiB};
data.totals=totals;
const marker='\n<!-- REFEREE_B_DATA_BEGIN -->\n';
const original=fs.readFileSync(REPORT,'utf8').split(marker)[0];
fs.writeFileSync(REPORT,original+marker+'\n## Exact numerical data\n\n```json\n'+JSON.stringify(data,null,2)+'\n```\n');
process.stdout.write(JSON.stringify({totals,constants:data.constants})+'\n');
```

## Numerical verdict

OPEN at the report level. The isolated referee B numerical task is complete:
**no counterexample to the stated proposition or its effective theorem was found.**
This is a finite numerical check, not a proof, kernel formalization, cross-vendor
review, application result, or novelty assessment.

| p | group order | samples | center-capped samples | largest capped ratio | p^(-1/60) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 3 | 24 | 85 | 82 | 1 | 0.981856409 |
| 5 | 120 | 90 | 87 | 0.898474581 | 0.973532602 |
| 7 | 336 | 96 | 93 | 0.878459703 | 0.968088437 |
| 11 | 1320 | 108 | 105 | 0.852541424 | 0.960823143 |
| 13 | 2184 | 114 | 111 | 0.843142093 | 0.958151707 |
| 17 | 4896 | 126 | 123 | 0.828125686 | 0.953877309 |
| 31 | 29760 | 168 | 165 | 0.795491090 | 0.944373891 |

- There were 787 measures in total, including 304 nonsymmetric measures.
- All 787 passed the exact universal-bracket comparison. For p<25 that bracket
  is always greater than 1, so these particular bracket checks are weak. At p=31
  and mu(Z)=0 it is 25/31 < 1, giving a nonvacuous bracket check.
- All 684 center-capped samples with p>=5 passed the exact p^(-1/60) comparison.
  This observation does not remove or lower the theorem's stated 2^40 onset.
- The one capped failure is the uniform law on Q8 inside SL(2,F_3): mu(Z)=1/4,
  t(mu)=t(mu*mu)=1/12, and the ratio is exactly 1. This is outside p>=5 and below
  onset. It is not a theorem counterexample.
- The three uncapped controls at each prime (delta at either scalar and the
  uniform law on Z) have ratio 1 and fail the power target as expected.
- The random/adversarial center-capped laws happened also to pass the necessary
  bound on every coset of Z. That is not the full all-proper-subgroup-coset
  hypothesis. The Q8 example explicitly fails that full hypothesis, since its
  mass on the proper subgroup Q8 itself is 1. Only the center-mass condition was
  used to classify the power-target test results.
- Each prime includes the uniform group law. Its zero energy and zero convolution
  energy are checked explicitly; no ratio is formed in that case.

The supplied exponent conversions are consistent: gamma=2c=1/60;
